{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Hf7smVJ6qwWqw9jBdqRT5Q","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Bootstrap From Scratch\n3\t * ============================================================================\n4\t *\n5\t * Proves that the fresh-volume init pipeline — `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/*`\n6\t * + every service's TypeORM migration chain — produces a clean, complete\n7\t * platform schema when run against an empty Postgres.\n8\t *\n9\t * # Why this test exists\n10\t *\n11\t * The codebase's bootstrap pipeline silently broke when several\n12\t * `CREATE TABLE` migrations were squashed out of source. Production survived\n13\t * because nobody did a fresh-volume init for months — every existing\n14\t * environment had the squashed-out tables already on disk. The first\n15\t * fresh-volume deploy crashed at `1781100000000-ConvertTimestampToTimestamptz`\n16\t * trying to ALTER `auth.users.mfaLockedUntil` (a column whose CREATE was\n17\t * gone). That is the exact regression class this invariant guards against.\n18\t *\n19\t * # What it does\n20\t *\n21\t *   1. Spin up `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` (the production image, by\n22\t *      digest pinned to docker-compose.infra.yml).\n23\t *   2. Copy `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/` into\n24\t *      `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` — Postgres runs every script in\n25\t *      lexical order on first boot of an empty PGDATA volume.\n26\t *   3. Connect, then for every service that owns a TypeORM migration\n27\t *      directory, dynamically load every migration class and run them\n28\t *      through a per-service DataSource bound to that service's schema.\n29\t *   4. Assert schema-level invariants: schemas exist, the previously\n30\t *      missing baseline tables now exist, the previously missing\n31\t *      `auth.users` columns now exist, the sensor hypertable was\n32\t *      created, db-migrate can owner-align a real tenant's continuous\n33\t *      aggregates, and each service's migration ledger is non-empty\n34\t *      (proving the migrations actually ran).\n35\t *\n36\t * # When this test fails\n37\t *\n38\t *   - A migration that creates a baseline table was squashed →\n39\t *     restore the CREATE in the earliest service migration. (This is\n40\t *     the original regression class.)\n41\t *   - An init-script SQL file was deleted without an equivalent\n42\t *     migration → either restore the script OR write a migration that\n43\t *     creates the same tables idempotently.\n44\t *   - A new entity was added without a corresponding CREATE TABLE\n45\t *     migration AND without an init-script entry → add the migration.\n46\t *   - The init scripts assume a privilege the bootstrap POSTGRES_USER\n47\t *     does not have on a fresh container → fix the script to use\n48\t *     a less-privileged path or supply additional bootstrap material.\n49\t *\n50\t * # Why testcontainers (not the shared TestDatabase pool)\n51\t *\n52\t * The other e2e/integration specs assert state of an *already-running*\n53\t * database that the dev/CI infra brought up — they're checking\n54\t * application-level behaviour. This test asserts the bootstrap ITSELF\n55\t * works on an EMPTY volume. It MUST start with no PGDATA, run the init\n56\t * scripts, then run the migration chain. testcontainers is the only\n57\t * way to get that hermetic guarantee inside a Jest worker.\n58\t *\n59\t * # Runtime\n60\t *\n61\t * ~3-5 minutes on a cold runner: image pull + init-script execution +\n62\t * 100+ migrations across 13 services. CI gate runs only on PRs that\n63\t * touch `apps/*\\/src/**\\/migrations/**` or\n64\t * `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/**` (see CI workflow\n65\t * `.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml` integration; if the gate\n66\t * is not yet wired, `npm run test:bootstrap` is the local entry point).\n67\t */\n68\t\n69\timport { readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from 'fs';\n70\timport { createRequire } from 'module';\n71\timport { join, resolve } from 'path';\n72\t\n73\timport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n74\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n75\timport { GenericContainer, type StartedTestContainer, Wait } from 'testcontainers';\n76\timport { DataSource, type MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n77\t\n78\t// ADR-031 Platform Bootstrap Atom — runs the schema/role/extension/function/\n79\t// shared-table DDL contract that init-scripts USED to own pre-cutover. The\n80\t// init-scripts directory copied below now contains only `01-init-databases.sql`\n81\t// (initdb-only DB GRANTs). Schemas, roles, functions, and shared.* tables\n82\t// are created by the call to runPlatformBootstrap() below before any\n83\t// per-service migration loop runs.\n84\timport {\n85\t  runPlatformBootstrap,\n86\t  resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir,\n87\t} from '../platform-bootstrap.service';\n88\timport { ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority } from '../tenant-sensor-continuous-aggregate-authority';\n89\t\n90\t// Constructor type for the @Entity / migration classes this spec loads\n91\t// dynamically. TypeORM's DataSource `entities`/`migrations` options accept\n92\t// `Function`, but `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-function-type` (correctly)\n93\t// rejects the bare `Function` type; a precise construct signature is both\n94\t// assignable to TypeORM's `Function` slot AND keeps the collected values\n95\t// typed as classes rather than arbitrary callables.\n96\ttype EntityClass = new (...args: never[]) => object;\n97\t\n98\t// Synchronous module loader bound to this spec's location. `import`/`await\n99\t// import()` cannot be used here: loadMigrationClassesFromDir is invoked inside\n100\t// `it.each(...)` arguments that Jest evaluates synchronously at collection\n101\t// time, so the load must be synchronous. `createRequire(__filename)` resolves\n102\t// migration classes and the cross-package schema-drift-validator through\n103\t// ts-jest at runtime, so the static type-check graph never reaches across\n104\t// project rootDirs — the same dynamic-load pattern the db-migrate\n105\t// platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts relies on.\n106\tconst requireModule = createRequire(__filename);\n107\t\n108\t// Structured logger for the (rare) entity-load skip path — `console.*` is\n109\t// banned platform-wide; the NestJS Logger keeps the diagnostic on the same\n110\t// structured-logging path the runtime services use.\n111\tconst logger = new Logger('bootstrap-from-scratch');\n112\t\n113\t// Local narrowing helper. db-migrate has @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion\n114\t// ENABLED (unlike the e2e project, whose assertDefined SSoT this replaces), so a\n115\t// bare `!` is banned here. assertDefined narrows `T | undefined` to `T` and fails\n116\t// loudly at the assertion site instead of surfacing as a cryptic\n117\t// \"cannot read properties of undefined\" further downstream.\n118\tfunction assertDefined<T>(value: T | null | undefined, message?: string): T {\n119\t  if (value === null || value === undefined) {\n120\t    throw new Error(message ?? 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8070c131-7617-49fa-b7e0-d0733efc07c0","uuid":"9364d157-a08a-47e9-86b8-1777986813cb","timestamp":"2026-10-08T01:54:32.196Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-bacb8bed08ee/apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * Bootstrap From Scratch\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * Proves that the fresh-volume init pipeline — `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/*`\n * + every service's TypeORM migration chain — produces a clean, complete\n * platform schema when run against an empty Postgres.\n *\n * # Why this test exists\n *\n * The codebase's bootstrap pipeline silently broke when several\n * `CREATE TABLE` migrations were squashed out of source. Production survived\n * because nobody did a fresh-volume init for months — every existing\n * environment had the squashed-out tables already on disk. The first\n * fresh-volume deploy crashed at `1781100000000-ConvertTimestampToTimestamptz`\n * trying to ALTER `auth.users.mfaLockedUntil` (a column whose CREATE was\n * gone). That is the exact regression class this invariant guards against.\n *\n * # What it does\n *\n *   1. Spin up `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` (the production image, by\n *      digest pinned to docker-compose.infra.yml).\n *   2. Copy `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/` into\n *      `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` — Postgres runs every script in\n *      lexical order on first boot of an empty PGDATA volume.\n *   3. Connect, then for every service that owns a TypeORM migration\n *      directory, dynamically load every migration class and run them\n *      through a per-service DataSource bound to that service's schema.\n *   4. Assert schema-level invariants: schemas exist, the previously\n *      missing baseline tables now exist, the previously missing\n *      `auth.users` columns now exist, the sensor hypertable was\n *      created, db-migrate can owner-align a real tenant's continuous\n *      aggregates, and each service's migration ledger is non-empty\n *      (proving the migrations actually ran).\n *\n * # When this test fails\n *\n *   - A migration that creates a baseline table was squashed →\n *     restore the CREATE in the earliest service migration. (This is\n *     the original regression class.)\n *   - An init-script SQL file was deleted without an equivalent\n *     migration → either restore the script OR write a migration that\n *     creates the same tables idempotently.\n *   - A new entity was added without a corresponding CREATE TABLE\n *     migration AND without an init-script entry → add the migration.\n *   - The init scripts assume a privilege the bootstrap POSTGRES_USER\n *     does not have on a fresh container → fix the script to use\n *     a less-privileged path or supply additional bootstrap material.\n *\n * # Why testcontainers (not the shared TestDatabase pool)\n *\n * The other e2e/integration specs assert state of an *already-running*\n * database that the dev/CI infra brought up — they're checking\n * application-level behaviour. This test asserts the bootstrap ITSELF\n * works on an EMPTY volume. It MUST start with no PGDATA, run the init\n * scripts, then run the migration chain. testcontainers is the only\n * way to get that hermetic guarantee inside a Jest worker.\n *\n * # Runtime\n *\n * ~3-5 minutes on a cold runner: image pull + init-script execution +\n * 100+ migrations across 13 services. CI gate runs only on PRs that\n * touch `apps/*\\/src/**\\/migrations/**` or\n * `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/**` (see CI workflow\n * `.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml` integration; if the gate\n * is not yet wired, `npm run test:bootstrap` is the local entry point).\n */\n\nimport { readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from 'fs';\nimport { createRequire } from 'module';\nimport { join, resolve } from 'path';\n\nimport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\nimport { GenericContainer, type StartedTestContainer, Wait } from 'testcontainers';\nimport { DataSource, type MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n\n// ADR-031 Platform Bootstrap Atom — runs the schema/role/extension/function/\n// shared-table DDL contract that init-scripts USED to own pre-cutover. The\n// init-scripts directory copied below now contains only `01-init-databases.sql`\n// (initdb-only DB GRANTs). Schemas, roles, functions, and shared.* tables\n// are created by the call to runPlatformBootstrap() below before any\n// per-service migration loop runs.\nimport {\n  runPlatformBootstrap,\n  resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir,\n} from '../platform-bootstrap.service';\nimport { ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority } from '../tenant-sensor-continuous-aggregate-authority';\n\n// Constructor type for the @Entity / migration classes this spec loads\n// dynamically. TypeORM's DataSource `entities`/`migrations` options accept\n// `Function`, but `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-function-type` (correctly)\n// rejects the bare `Function` type; a precise construct signature is both\n// assignable to TypeORM's `Function` slot AND keeps the collected values\n// typed as classes rather than arbitrary callables.\ntype EntityClass = new (...args: never[]) => object;\n\n// Synchronous module loader bound to this spec's location. `import`/`await\n// import()` cannot be used here: loadMigrationClassesFromDir is invoked inside\n// `it.each(...)` arguments that Jest evaluates synchronously at collection\n// time, so the load must be synchronous. `createRequire(__filename)` resolves\n// migration classes and the cross-package schema-drift-validator through\n// ts-jest at runtime, so the static type-check graph never reaches across\n// project rootDirs — the same dynamic-load pattern the db-migrate\n// platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts relies on.\nconst requireModule = createRequire(__filename);\n\n// Structured logger for the (rare) entity-load skip path — `console.*` is\n// banned platform-wide; the NestJS Logger keeps the diagnostic on the same\n// structured-logging path the runtime services use.\nconst logger = new Logger('bootstrap-from-scratch');\n\n// Local narrowing helper. db-migrate has @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion\n// ENABLED (unlike the e2e project, whose assertDefined SSoT this replaces), so a\n// bare `!` is banned here. assertDefined narrows `T | undefined` to `T` and fails\n// loudly at the assertion site instead of surfacing as a cryptic\n// \"cannot read properties of undefined\" further downstream.\nfunction assertDefined<T>(value: T | null | undefined, message?: string): T {\n  if (value === null || value === undefined) {\n    throw new Error(message ?? 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');","numLines":120,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1416}}}

Importing\n126\t// schema-drift-validator.service.ts directly via `import { ... }` would pull a\n127\t// file from another Nx project into db-migrate's type-check graph. ts-jest\n128\t// resolves this lazily at runtime (same pattern the migration loader uses for\n129\t// migration classes), so the validator's stable runtime contract is exercised\n130\t// without a static cross-project import. From this spec's location\n131\t// (apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/) the repo root is four levels up.\n132\ttype SchemaDriftValidatorFactory = (\n133\t  serviceName: string,\n134\t) => new (ds: DataSource, cs: ConfigService) => { onApplicationBootstrap(): Promise<void> };\n135\tfunction loadDriftValidatorFactory(): SchemaDriftValidatorFactory {\n136\t  const mod = requireModule(\n137\t    '../../../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-drift-validator.service',\n138\t  ) as {\n139\t    createSchemaDriftValidator: SchemaDriftValidatorFactory;\n140\t  };\n141\t  return mod.createSchemaDriftValidator;\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\t// The same image+digest the production stack uses — see\n145\t// docker-compose.infra.yml line 12. Pinning by digest guarantees the\n146\t// init-script behaviour we're proving here matches what runs in\n147\t// production. Bumping the digest is a deliberate review surface.\n148\tconst POSTGRES_IMAGE =\n149\t  'timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7';\n150\t\n151\tconst DATABASE_NAME = 'aquaculture';\n152\tconst DATABASE_USER = 'aquaculture';\n153\tconst DATABASE_PASSWORD = 'aquaculture-test';\n154\tconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\n155\tconst SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS = [\n156\t  'AUTH_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n157\t  'FARM_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n158\t  'SENSOR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n159\t  'BILLING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n160\t  'HR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n161\t  'ALERT_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n162\t  'ADMIN_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n163\t  'GATEWAY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n164\t  'NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n165\t  'HYDROPONICS_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n166\t  'AI_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n167\t  'MESSAGING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n168\t  'OBSERVABILITY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n169\t  'EVENT_STORE_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n170\t  'CONFIG_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n171\t] as const;\n172\t\n173\t// Repo root, derived from this file's location:\n174\t//   apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts -> ../../../..\n175\t// (mirrors how the sibling platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts resolves it).\n176\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..');\n177\tconst INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR = join(REPO_ROOT, 'infrastructure', 'docker', 'init-scripts');\n178\t\n179\tasync function runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n180\t  const previous = process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV];\n181\t  process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = '1';\n182\t  try {\n183\t    return await operation();\n184\t  } finally {\n185\t    if (previous === undefined) {\n186\t      Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env, DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV);\n187\t    } else {\n188\t      process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = previous;\n189\t    }\n190\t  }\n191\t}\n192\t\n193\t/**\n194\t * Per-service migration manifest.\n195\t *\n196\t * Each entry maps a service to:\n197\t *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n198\t *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n199\t *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n200\t *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n201\t *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n202\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n203\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n204\t *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n205\t *     when adding a service.\n206\t *\n207\t * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n208\t * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n209\t * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n210\t * else is alphabetical.\n211\t */\n212\tinterface ServiceManifest {\n213\t  name: string;\n214\t  schema: string;\n215\t  migrationsDir: string;\n216\t  /**\n217\t   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n218\t   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n219\t   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n220\t   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n221\t   * exists in the DB. Services without entities (gateway-api) leave it\n222\t   * unset.\n223\t   */\n224\t  entitiesGlob?: string;\n225\t}\n226\t\n227\tconst SERVICES: ServiceManifest[] = [\n228\t  // auth FIRST — every other service's FKs reference auth.tenants.\n229\t  {\n230\t    name: 'auth-service',\n231\t    schema: 'auth',\n232\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations',\n233\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/auth-service/src',\n234\t  },\n235\t  // Then the rest, ordered to minimize cross-schema FK ordering hazards.\n236\t  {\n237\t    name: 'farm-service',\n238\t    schema: 'farm',\n239\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations',\n240\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/farm-service/src',\n241\t  },\n242\t  {\n243\t    name: 'sensor-service',\n244\t    schema: 'sensor',\n245\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations',\n246\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/sensor-service/src',\n247\t  },\n248\t  {\n249\t    name: 'hr-service',\n250\t    schema: 'hr',\n251\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations',\n252\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hr-service/src',\n253\t  },\n254\t  {\n255\t    name: 'messaging-service',\n256\t    schema: 'messaging',\n257\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations',\n258\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/messaging-service/src',\n259\t  },\n260\t  {\n261\t    name: 'alert-engine',\n262\t    schema: 'alert',\n263\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations',\n264\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/alert-engine/src',\n265\t  },\n266\t  {\n267\t    name: 'billing-service',\n268\t    schema: 'billing',\n269\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/billing-service/src/database/migrations',\n270\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/billing-service/src',\n271\t  },\n272\t  {\n273\t    name: 'notification-service',\n274\t    schema: 'notification',\n275\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/notification-service/src/database/migrations',\n276\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/notification-service/src',\n277\t  },\n278\t  {\n279\t    name: 'observability-service',\n280\t    schema: 'observability',\n281\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/observability-service/src/database/migrations',\n282\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/observability-service/src',\n283\t  },\n284\t  {\n285\t    name: 'admin-api-service',\n286\t    schema: 'admin',\n287\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations',\n288\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/admin-api-service/src',\n289\t  },\n290\t  {\n291\t    name: 'event-store-service',\n292\t    schema: 'event_store',\n293\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/event-store-service/src/migrations',\n294\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/event-store-service/src',\n295\t  },\n296\t  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — close the manifest gap. The 4 services\n297\t  // below were absent from the original 11-service manifest, so the\n298\t  // bootstrap test passed even when their migration chains were empty\n299\t  // or their entity-surface drifted from the on-disk schema. Migrations dir is\n305\t  //     intentionally empty (.gitkeep only) — the entity-surface\n306\t  //     check still runs, the migration runner is a no-op.\n307\t  //   gateway-api — pure HTTP fronting, owns NO entities and NO\n308\t  //     migrations. 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');\n  }\n  return value;\n}\n\n// Dynamic load of the backend-common schema-drift-validator. Importing\n// schema-drift-validator.service.ts directly via `import { ... }` would pull a\n// file from another Nx project into db-migrate's type-check graph. ts-jest\n// resolves this lazily at runtime (same pattern the migration loader uses for\n// migration classes), so the validator's stable runtime contract is exercised\n// without a static cross-project import. From this spec's location\n// (apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/) the repo root is four levels up.\ntype SchemaDriftValidatorFactory = (\n  serviceName: string,\n) => new (ds: DataSource, cs: ConfigService) => { onApplicationBootstrap(): Promise<void> };\nfunction loadDriftValidatorFactory(): SchemaDriftValidatorFactory {\n  const mod = requireModule(\n    '../../../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-drift-validator.service',\n  ) as {\n    createSchemaDriftValidator: SchemaDriftValidatorFactory;\n  };\n  return mod.createSchemaDriftValidator;\n}\n\n// The same image+digest the production stack uses — see\n// docker-compose.infra.yml line 12. Pinning by digest guarantees the\n// init-script behaviour we're proving here matches what runs in\n// production. Bumping the digest is a deliberate review surface.\nconst POSTGRES_IMAGE =\n  'timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7';\n\nconst DATABASE_NAME = 'aquaculture';\nconst DATABASE_USER = 'aquaculture';\nconst DATABASE_PASSWORD = 'aquaculture-test';\nconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\nconst SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS = [\n  'AUTH_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'FARM_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'SENSOR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'BILLING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'HR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'ALERT_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'ADMIN_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'GATEWAY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'HYDROPONICS_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'AI_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'MESSAGING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'OBSERVABILITY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'EVENT_STORE_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'CONFIG_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n] as const;\n\n// Repo root, derived from this file's location:\n//   apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts -> ../../../..\n// (mirrors how the sibling platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts resolves it).\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..');\nconst INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR = join(REPO_ROOT, 'infrastructure', 'docker', 'init-scripts');\n\nasync function runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  const previous = process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV];\n  process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = '1';\n  try {\n    return await operation();\n  } finally {\n    if (previous === undefined) {\n      Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env, DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV);\n    } else {\n      process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = previous;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Per-service migration manifest.\n *\n * Each entry maps a service to:\n *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n *     when adding a service.\n *\n * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n * else is alphabetical.\n */\ninterface ServiceManifest {\n  name: string;\n  schema: string;\n  migrationsDir: string;\n  /**\n   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n   * exists in the DB. Services without entities (gateway-api) leave it\n   * unset.\n   */\n  entitiesGlob?: string;\n}\n\nconst SERVICES: ServiceManifest[] = [\n  // auth FIRST — every other service's FKs reference auth.tenants.\n  {\n    name: 'auth-service',\n    schema: 'auth',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/auth-service/src',\n  },\n  // Then the rest, ordered to minimize cross-schema FK ordering hazards.\n  {\n    name: 'farm-service',\n    schema: 'farm',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/farm-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'sensor-service',\n    schema: 'sensor',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/sensor-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'hr-service',\n    schema: 'hr',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hr-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'messaging-service',\n    schema: 'messaging',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/messaging-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'alert-engine',\n    schema: 'alert',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/alert-engine/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'billing-service',\n    schema: 'billing',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/billing-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/billing-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'notification-service',\n    schema: 'notification',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/notification-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/notification-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'observability-service',\n    schema: 'observability',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/observability-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/observability-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'admin-api-service',\n    schema: 'admin',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/admin-api-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'event-store-service',\n    schema: 'event_store',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/event-store-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/event-store-service/src',\n  },\n  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — close the manifest gap. The 4 services\n  // below were absent from the original 11-service manifest, so the\n  // bootstrap test passed even when their migration chains were empty\n  // or their entity-surface drifted from the on-disk schema. Adding\n  // them here closes the regression class:\n  //\n  //   hydroponics-service / ai-service — schema-per-tenant services\n  //     with their own baseline migrations + entity surface.\n  //   config-service — platform-level service. Migrations dir is\n  //     intentionally empty (.gitkeep only) — the entity-surface\n  //     check still runs, the migration runner is a no-op.\n  //   gateway-api — pure HTTP fronting, owns NO entities and NO\n  //     migrations. Listed in the manifest so the schema-ownership\n  //     audit (Part B B.4) can assert the `gateway` schema exists","numLines":190,"startLine":120,"totalLines":1416}}}

The finding is a conjunction: (A) this is a hazardous migration, and (B) it has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal. (A) is verifiable inside the cited file and I confirm it: line 24 declares a TypeORM MigrationInterface, line 27 opens up(), line 28 pins the search_path, line 31 issues CREATE TABLE auth.auth_outbox, line 65 adds a UNIQUE partial index, and line 71 opens a down() that drops the table and its indexes. (B) is an ABSENCE claim, and the one thing an absence claim cannot be read from is the file the absence is about. Proving it requires enumerating the mechanisms by which this repository actually covers a migration, and then showing each one misses. I enumerated them and the opposite is true. FIRST MECHANISM, AND THE DISPOSITIVE ONE: apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts spins up the digest-pinned production Postgres image and, per its own contract at lines 26-34, for every service owning a migration directory dynamically loads every migration class and runs them, then asserts each service's migration ledger is non-empty so that the migrations provably executed. Its SERVICES manifest at lines 227-234 carries 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations' as the FIRST entry, with entitiesGlob set so the entity-surface-vs-DB matrix also asserts the AuthOutbox table surface exists. That is the subject file's own directory. Its up() is therefore executed, and the columns created at lines 31-47 are asserted against the entity surface, by a test. SECOND: .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml path-triggers on 'apps/*/src/**/migrations/**' at line 91, which matches this file exactly, and its bootstrap-from-scratch job at lines 273-320 runs that spec via npm run test:bootstrap on PR, on push to main, and nightly. The gate is wired, not merely declared. THIRD: the same workflow runs three witness gates whose whole subject is migration files -- migration-deletion-witness, migration-immutability-witness and entity-diff-witness at lines 586-652 -- plus shell checks at lines 142-232 that find every apps/**/migrations/*.ts and enforce filename, class-name and per-service timestamp uniqueness. FOURTH: e2e/tests/integration/schema-invariants.spec.ts B.5b at lines 710-744 dynamically enumerates every BASE TABLE in the auth schema, which includes auth_outbox once applied, and asserts zero tenant_<uuid> clones -- precisely the invariant the @SourceOnlyMigration decorator declares at lines 20-23 -- and the workflow executes it at lines 474-477 and 570-573 against a database the real db-migrate entry point has just migrated. WHY THE DETECTOR WAS WRONG, MECHANICALLY. Coverage here is reached by createRequire plus a readdirSync directory scan (spec lines 69-71 and 106), which emits NO static import edge, and migrations are registered by glob rather than by a barrel -- apps/auth-service/src/database/data-source.ts:34 is 'migrations: [src/migrations/[0-9]*.ts]' and no src/migrations/index.ts exists. A rule that looks for an adjacent *.spec.ts or a static import edge is structurally blind to both. The adjacency signal is genuinely absent; the coverage it is used to proxy for is present. A detector finding whose literal output is true but whose asserted defect does not exist in the repository is a false positive, and that is this one. WHAT BREAKS IF THIS IS RATIFIED. The pair's agreement is about to suppress a finding class, quarantine rules and score judges. Ratifying it teaches the suppression surface that this repository's deliberately centralized migration-coverage architecture is a gap, which will reproduce on every migration in the same manifest. The decision memory already shows the signature: migration-without-test findings on hr-service Baseline, hr-service CreateHrFinanceTables, alert-engine Baseline and hr-service DropPayrollCurrencyDefault all escalated to HUMAN_REQUIRED on judge_disagreement or low_confidence. Those services' migration directories sit in the same manifest (hr-service at lines 249-253, alert-engine at lines 260-265). (A) is verifiable inside the cited file and I confirm it: line 24 declares a TypeORM MigrationInterface, line 27 opens up(), line 28 pins the search_path, line 31 issues CREATE TABLE auth.auth_outbox, line 65 adds a UNIQUE partial index, and line 71 opens a down() that drops the table and its indexes. (B) is an ABSENCE claim, and the one thing an absence claim cannot be read from is the file the absence is about. Proving it requires enumerating the mechanisms by which this repository actually covers a migration, and then showing each one misses. I enumerated them and the opposite is true. FIRST MECHANISM, AND THE DISPOSITIVE ONE: apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts spins up the digest-pinned production Postgres image and, per its own contract at lines 26-34, for every service owning a migration directory dynamically loads every migration class and runs them, then asserts each service's migration ledger is non-empty so that the migrations provably executed. Its SERVICES manifest at lines 227-234 carries 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations' as the FIRST entry, with entitiesGlob set so the entity-surface-vs-DB matrix also asserts the AuthOutbox table surface exists. That is the subject file's own directory. Its up() is therefore executed, and the columns created at lines 31-47 are asserted against the entity surface, by a test. SECOND: .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml path-triggers on 'apps/*/src/**/migrations/**' at line 91, which matches this file exactly, and its bootstrap-from-scratch job at lines 273-320 runs that spec via npm run test:bootstrap on PR, on push to main, and nightly. The gate is wired, not merely declared. THIRD: the same workflow runs three witness gates whose whole subject is migration files -- migration-deletion-witness, migration-immutability-witness and entity-diff-witness at lines 586-652 -- plus shell checks at lines 142-232 that find every apps/**/migrations/*.ts and enforce filename, class-name and per-service timestamp uniqueness. FOURTH: e2e/tests/integration/schema-invariants.spec.ts B.5b at lines 710-744 dynamically enumerates every BASE TABLE in the auth schema, which includes auth_outbox once applied, and asserts zero tenant_<uuid> clones -- precisely the invariant the @SourceOnlyMigration decorator declares at lines 20-23 -- and the workflow executes it at lines 474-477 and 570-573 against a database the real db-migrate entry point has just migrated. WHY THE DETECTOR WAS WRONG, MECHANICALLY. Coverage here is reached by createRequire plus a readdirSync directory scan (spec lines 69-71 and 106), which emits NO static import edge, and migrations are registered by glob rather than by a barrel -- apps/auth-service/src/database/data-source.ts:34 is 'migrations: [src/migrations/[0-9]*.ts]' and no src/migrations/index.ts exists. A rule that looks for an adjacent *.spec.ts or a static import edge is structurally blind to both. The adjacency signal is genuinely absent; the coverage it is used to proxy for is present. A detector finding whose literal output is true but whose asserted defect does not exist in the repository is a false positive, and that is this one. WHAT BREAKS IF THIS IS RATIFIED. The pair's agreement is about to suppress a finding class, quarantine rules and score judges. Ratifying it teaches the suppression surface that this repository's deliberately centralized migration-coverage architecture is a gap, which will reproduce on every migration in the same manifest. Those services' migration directories sit in the same manifest (hr-service at lines 249-253, alert-engine at lines 260-265).
